Completed means the initial curated cross-reference pass: the source verse and every listed target passage were read in the pinned Saints-Translation English records, and each retained pair has a specific explanation. 1 Chronicles 1:1

Adam, Seth, Enosh,

  1. Genesis 5:3-6 genealogical parallel

    Both place Seth after Adam and Enosh after Seth in the ancestral line.

    Read matching passage

    Genesis 5:3 And Adam lived two hundred and thirty years, and begot a son in his own likeness and image, and named him Seth.

    Genesis 5:4 And the days of Adam after he begot Seth were seven hundred years, and he begot sons and daughters.

    Genesis 5:5 And all the days that Adam lived were nine hundred and thirty years, and he died.

    Genesis 5:6 And Seth lived two hundred and five years, and begot Enosh.

    Pinned source record

  1. Genesis 5:21-25 genealogical parallel

    Both ancestral sequences name Enoch, Methuselah, and Lamech in that order.

    Read matching passage

    Genesis 5:21 And Enoch lived one hundred and sixty-five years, and begot Methuselah.

    Genesis 5:22 And Enoch pleased God for two hundred years after he begot Methuselah, and begot sons and daughters.

    Genesis 5:23 And all the days of Enoch were three hundred and sixty-five years.

    Genesis 5:24 And Enoch pleased God, and was not found, for God transferred him.

    Genesis 5:25 And Methuselah lived one hundred and sixty-seven years, and begot Lamech.

    Both say Abraham begot Isaac and identify Esau and Jacob as Isaac's sons.

    Read matching passage

    Genesis 25:19 And these are the generations of Isaac, Abraham’s son: Abraham begot Isaac.

    Genesis 25:20 And Isaac was forty years old when he took Rebekah, the daughter of Bethuel the Syrian from Mesopotamia, the sister of Laban the Syrian, to be his wife.

    Genesis 25:21 And Isaac prayed to the Lord concerning Rebekah his wife, because she was barren; and God heard him, and Rebekah his wife conceived.

    Genesis 25:22 And the children leaped within her; and she said, “If it is to be this way with me, why is this happening to me?” And she went to inquire of the Lord.

    Genesis 25:23 And the Lord said to her, “Two nations are in your womb, and two peoples shall be separated from within you; and one people shall surpass the other, and the greater shall serve the lesser.”

    Genesis 25:24 And her days to give birth were fulfilled, and behold, there were twins in her womb.

    Genesis 25:25 And the firstborn son came out red, all over like a hairy hide; and he was named Esau.

    Genesis 25:26 And afterward his brother came out, and his hand held Esau’s heel; and he was named Jacob. And Isaac was sixty years old when Rebekah bore them.

1 Chronicles 2:3

Judah’s sons: Er, Onan, Shelon, three. They were born to him by Shua’s daughter, the Canaanite woman. Er, Judah’s firstborn, was evil before the Lord, and He killed him.

  1. Genesis 38:2-7 bounded genealogical and narrative parallel

    Both record Judah's children in a Canaanite marriage, name Er and Onan, and say the evil firstborn Er was put to death by God. The target's wording of Shua's relationship and Shelon/Shelah remain distinct.

    Read matching passage

    Genesis 38:2 And Judah saw there a Canaanite man’s daughter named Shua; and he took her, and went in to her.

    Genesis 38:3 And she conceived and bore a son, and called his name Er.

    Genesis 38:4 And she conceived again and bore a son, and called his name Onan.

    Genesis 38:5 And she bore another son, and called his name Shelah. And she was in Chasbi when she bore them.

    Genesis 38:6 And Judah took a wife for Er his firstborn, whose name was Tamar.

    Genesis 38:7 And Er, Judah’s firstborn, was evil before the Lord, and God put him to death.

    Pinned source record

1 Chronicles 2:4

Tamar his daughter-in-law bore him Phares and Zara. Judah’s sons were five in all.

  1. Genesis 38:24-30 bounded genealogical and narrative parallel

    Both describe Tamar, Judah's daughter-in-law, bearing his twin sons; Phares/Perez and Zara/Zerah remain differing names rather than independently resolved identities.

    Read matching passage

    Genesis 38:24 And after three months Judah was told, “Tamar your daughter-in-law has acted as a prostitute, and behold, she is with child through her prostitution.” And Judah said, “Bring her out, and let her be burned.”

    Genesis 38:25 And as she was being brought out, she sent to her father-in-law, saying, “I am with child by the man to whom these belong.” And she said, “See to whom this ring, and this necklace, and this staff belong.”

    Genesis 38:26 And Judah recognized them, and said, “Tamar is more in the right than I, because I did not give her to Shelah my son.” And he knew her no more.

    Genesis 38:27 And when she gave birth, behold, there were twins in her womb.

    Genesis 38:28 And as she was giving birth, one put out his hand; and the midwife took it and bound a scarlet thread upon his hand, saying, “This one shall come out first.”

    Genesis 38:29 But as he drew back his hand, immediately his brother came out; and she said, “Why has a barrier been broken through because of you?” And his name was called Perez.

    Genesis 38:30 And afterward his brother came out, upon whose hand was the scarlet thread; and his name was called Zerah.

    Pinned source record

    The closing notice attributes the nations’ fear of David to the Lord; the preceding inquiry and victory expressly give the enemies into David’s hands and attribute the breakthrough to God, explaining the narrated basis for his growing reputation.
